0 2.83Z\"\u003e\u003c/path\u003e\u003c/svg\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eTau Ceti is a repository of formal mathematics with human-directed and human-reviewed roadmaps,\nimplemented and maintained by AI contributors, subject to adversarial review.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eTau Ceti is being incubated by the \u003ca href=\"https://lean-lang.org/fro/\" rel=\"nofollow\"\u003eLean FRO\u003c/a\u003e and the \u003ca href=\"https://https://mathlib-initiative.org/\" rel=\"nofollow\"\u003eMathlib Initiative\u003c/a\u003e\nin partnership with academic and industry groups.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eOur goal is to formalize as much mathematics as we can in a collaborative, coherent library,\nat the highest quality we can, subject to the constraint that everything is written by AIs. It's an experiment, and could use your help!\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eWe hope that by building Tau Ceti we can ensure that a significant part of AI formalization work is performed in an open-source, human directed library. Tau Ceti will be built for reuse and generality. Tau Ceti is a community resource, licensed under the Apache licence, that everyone can build on top of.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eWe've long dreamt about formalizing all the \"basic material\" in mathematics.\nWhile we're explicitly \u003cstrong\u003enot\u003c/strong\u003e aiming here at curating and digesting mathematical knowledge in the way that a human authored library like \u003ca href=\"https://leanprover-community.github.io/\" rel=\"nofollow\"\u003eMathlib\u003c/a\u003e can,\nwe hope that we can efficiently build a reusable library at significant scale. With Tau Ceti built, we'll be closer to the point where computers can genuinely help us explore the mathematical universe:\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cul dir=\"auto\"\u003e\n\u003cli\u003ethe Lean kernel verifies\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003ethe Lean language and user tactics provide automation in proof construction\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003eAIs assist with proof exploration\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003eand Mathlib, Tau Ceti, and other libraries provide the knowledge necessary so that humans can work at the research frontier.\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003c/ul\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eHumans own the roadmap for Tau Ceti, which lives in the\n\u003ca href=\"https://github.com/TauCetiProject/TauCetiRoadmap\"\u003eTauCetiRoadmap\u003c/a\u003e repository (mostly in the form of markdown files, together with a\nsmall amount of Lean); changes are made via human-reviewed pull requests there.\nRoadmap authors and reviewers can use AI assistance; see the\n\u003ca href=\"https://github.com/TauCetiProject/TauCetiRoadmap/blob/main/CONTRIBUTING.md\"\u003econtribution guide\u003c/a\u003e.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eAIs own the code in this repository, initiating pull requests and shepherding them through an\nAI-driven review process.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eHumans can raise issues against the code, and leave implementation (and review) to AIs.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003cem\u003e(Tau Ceti is a sun-like star about 12 light years from our own, and a favourite setting for sci-fi stories.)\u003c/em\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"markdown-heading\" dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003ch2 tabindex=\"-1\" class=\"heading-element\" dir=\"auto\"\u003eThe three selects the newest successfully built Mathlib master commit\nwhose full cache it can verify. It opens one bot bump PR and keeps that target fixed while\nworkers repair TauCeti, saving partial source repairs between sessions. Full CI, axiom audits,\nreview, and Bors integration must pass before it lands. The next hourly run selects another\nrecent target. Downstream-reports remains external reporting and does not control these updates.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"markdown-heading\" dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003ch2 tabindex=\"-1\" class=\"heading-element\" dir=\"auto\"\u003eRelationship to Lean Pool\u003c/h2\u003e\u003ca id=\"user-content-relationship-to-lean-pool\" class=\"anchor\" aria-label=\"Permalink: Relationship to Lean Pool\" href=\"#relationship-to-lean-pool\"\u003e\u003csvg data-component=\"Octicon\" class=\"octicon octicon-link\" viewBox=\"0 0 16 16\" version=\"1.1\" width=\"16\" height=\"16\" aria-hidden=\"true\"\u003e\u003cpath d=\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z\"\u003e\u003c/path\u003e\u003c/svg\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eTau Ceti and \u003ca href=\"https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool\"\u003eLean Pool\u003c/a\u003e are complementary. Lean Pool is\nan arXiv/AfP-like archive of independent formalization projects, whether human- or AI-written.\nTau Ceti is an integrated, AI-built mathematical library whose contents follow human-audited\nroadmaps and undergo review for coherence, reuse, and compatibility with Mathlib.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"markdown-heading\" dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003ch2 tabindex=\"-1\" class=\"heading-element\" dir=\"auto\"\u003eReview\u003c/h2\u003e\u003ca id=\"user-content-review\" class=\"anchor\" aria-label=\"Permalink: Review\" href=\"#review\"\u003e\u003csvg data-component=\"Octicon\" class=\"octicon octicon-link\" viewBox=\"0 0 16 16\" version=\"1.1\" width=\"16\" height=\"16\" aria-hidden=\"true\"\u003e\u003cpath d=\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z\"\u003e\u003c/path\u003e\u003c/svg\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eReview of implementation PRs is entirely driven by AIs. These operate according to a fixed open source rubric. Humans write the rubric, and update it as the project evolves.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eWhen a PR is opened, CI normally checks changed modules: the full Mathlib environment-linter set, docstrings, suppression checks, and source style. This initial check is the PR preflight. Dot-notation checks reuse content-addressed parsing context while computing findings afresh. Batch builds, dependency bumps, and uncertain scope use staging checks: changed-module environment lint plus downstream unused-argument checks, and library-wide docstring, suppression, source-style, and dot-notation checks. The \u003ccode\u003efull-lint\u003c/code\u003e label and the daily sweep run every environment linter across the library; the daily sweep opens a repair PR if a change broke lint elsewhere. Once CI passes, a review can be run against the rubrics; its verdicts are posted as \"block\", \"changes requested\", or \"approval\".\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003ePR contributors can push further commits, or respond to review comments, in order to solicit updated reviews.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eWe've built the infrastructure to fire these reviews automatically on each PR (and on a \u003ccode\u003e/review\u003c/code\u003e comment), but it is currently switched off. For now, reviews are run from the command line.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eYou can also run the same review yourself from the command line, on your own Claude and/or Codex subscription instead of the project's metered API budget, using the \u003ccode\u003etauceti-review\u003c/code\u003e tool in \u003ca href=\"https://github.com/TauCetiProject/TauCetiReview\"\u003eTauCetiReview\u003c/a\u003e. With \u003ca href=\"https://docs.astral.sh/uv/\" rel=\"nofollow\"\u003euv\u003c/a\u003e:\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"highlight highlight-source-shell notranslate position-relative overflow-auto\" dir=\"auto\" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content=\"# print the verdicts for PR #42, posting nothing:\nuvx --from git+https://github.com/TauCetiProject/TauCetiReview tauceti-review 42\n# add --post to publish the scoreboard and per-rubric threads, as you:\nuvx --from git+https://github.com/TauCetiProject/TauCetiReview tauceti-review 42 --post\"\u003e\u003cpre\u003e\u003cspan class=\"pl-c\"\u003e\u003cspan class=\"pl-c\"\u003e#\u003c/span\u003e print the verdicts for PR #42, posting nothing:\u003c/span\u003e\nuvx --from git+https://github.com/TauCetiProject/TauCetiReview tauceti-review 42\n\u003cspan class=\"pl-c\"\u003e\u003cspan class=\"pl-c\"\u003e#\u003c/span\u003e add --post to publish the scoreboard and per-rubric threads, as you:\u003c/span\u003e\nuvx --from git+https://github.com/TauCetiProject/TauCetiReview tauceti-review 42 --post\u003c/pre\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eIt runs the identical engine and rubrics CI uses, in a clean room that ignores your personal editor configuration so the review stays reproducible. See \u003ca href=\"https://github.com/TauCetiProject/TauCetiReview/blob/main/REVIEWING.md\"\u003eREVIEWING.md\u003c/a\u003e for prerequisites, flags, and the contest/re-review flow.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eThe rubrics are \u003cstrong\u003eadversarial\u003c/strong\u003e, including instructions to find mis-formalizations, vacuous statements, and \"pushing around the lump in the carpet\". There are rubrics for many different aspects of review — scope, correctness, reuse, attribution, API design, generality, placement, naming, documentation, proof quality, and deprecation; see \u003ca href=\"https://github.com/TauCetiProject/TauCetiReview/tree/main/rubrics\"\u003ethe rubrics directory\u003c/a\u003e. We'll update these as we see what is most useful!\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eWe also have prototype systems for \"meta review\", using human and AI judges to do A/B testing of reviews, so that we can quantitatively evaluate review quality, and how models and rubrics feed into this quality.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eContent CI builds and audits the mathematics without running regression suites for\nthe CI machinery. \u003cstrong\u003eInfrastructure tests\u003c/strong\u003e runs those suites separately, only when\n\u003ccode\u003escripts/\u003c/code\u003e or \u003ccode\u003e.github/\u003c/code\u003e changes. Its results do not gate content builds, merge\nstatuses, lint, or cache publication.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eHuman-owned infrastructure changes still require human review. After reviewing a PR,\nrepository admins can issue \u003ccode\u003ebors r+\u003c/code\u003e or \u003ccode\u003ebors merge\u003c/code\u003e: bors records approval\nfor the accepted head and merge base, requests trusted CI, and owns testing and\nlanding the change.\nThis approval waives only the restricted-path rule; builds, audits, Lake configuration\nattestation and pin/size guards still apply. A push, changed merge base or \u003ccode\u003ebors r-\u003c/code\u003e\nrequires fresh approval. Both bors and CI check the approving account's current GitHub\nrepository admin role; removing that role invalidates its scope authority.\nOther users follow the existing review policy. See the\n\u003ca href=\"https://github.com/TauCetiProject/bors-ng/blob/master/deploy/manual-approval.md\"\u003ebors rollout and behavior documentation\u003c/a\u003e.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eStacked PRs are prohibited, including drafts: land prerequisites first, then rebase and\nsubmit a standalone diff. The trusted \u003ca href=\"/TauCetiProject/TauCeti/blob/main/scripts/stack-policy.md\"\u003eno-stacking policy\u003c/a\u003e\nchecks current source evidence, comments and closes confirmed stacks, and blocks their\nbors admission. Keep/hold/human labels do not exempt stacks. Reviewers should follow\nthe same policy; shared files, imports and roadmap areas alone do not establish stacking.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"markdown-heading\" dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003ch2 tabindex=\"-1\" class=\"heading-element\" dir=\"auto\"\u003eMathlib dependency\u003c/h2\u003e\u003ca id=\"user-content-mathlib-dependency\" class=\"anchor\" aria-label=\"Permalink: Mathlib dependency\" href=\"#mathlib-dependency\"\u003e\u003csvg data-component=\"Octicon\" class=\"octicon octicon-link\" viewBox=\"0 0 16 16\" version=\"1.1\" width=\"16\" height=\"16\" aria-hidden=\"true\"\u003e\u003cpath d=\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z\"\u003e\u003c/path\u003e\u003c/svg\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eAlthough Tau Ceti and \u003ca href=\"https://leanprover-community.github.io/\" rel=\"nofollow\"\u003eMathlib\u003c/a\u003e differ both in the review mechanisms and in design standards, and while they target different mathematical goals, we envision a strong synergy between the two libraries. We hope to build overlapping communities around both libraries.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eTau Ceti depends on Mathlib's \u003ccode\u003emaster\u003c/code\u003e branch, and always defers to design decisions made in Mathlib.\nAIs are encouraged to make PRs to Tau Ceti that bump the pin to new commits on Mathlib's \u003ccode\u003emaster\u003c/code\u003e branch, and fix any resulting problems in Tau Ceti.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eWe won't push material upstream from Tau Ceti to Mathlib. Mathlib contributors are welcome to adopt, curate, and modify material from Tau Ceti, while preparing PRs to Mathlib. Everything here is \u003ca href=\"http://www.apache.org/licenses/\" rel=\"nofollow\"\u003eApache licensed\u003c/a\u003e.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"markdown-heading\" dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003ch2 tabindex=\"-1\" class=\"heading-element\" dir=\"auto\"\u003eWhat Tau Ceti is, and is not\u003c/h2\u003e\u003ca id=\"user-content-what-tau-ceti-is-and-is-not\" class=\"anchor\" aria-label=\"Permalink: What Tau Ceti is, and is not\" href=\"#what-tau-ceti-is-and-is-not\"\u003e\u003csvg data-component=\"Octicon\" class=\"octicon octicon-link\" viewBox=\"0 0 16 16\" version=\"1.1\" width=\"16\" height=\"16\" aria-hidden=\"true\"\u003e\u003cpath d=\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z\"\u003e\u003c/path\u003e\u003c/svg\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cblockquote\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eThe product of mathematics is clarity and understanding. Not theorems, by themselves. ... There is no way to run out of ideas in need of clarification. The question of who is the first person to ever set foot on some square meter of land is really secondary. --- Bill Thurston\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003c/blockquote\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eThere are many reasons to work on formalizing mathematics, and everyone involved in formalization comes with different reasons. Here are some:\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003col dir=\"auto\"\u003e\n\u003cli\u003eTo enjoy the satisfying feeling of the 🎉 emoji when the computer accepts your proof.\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003eTo build a modern Bourbaki, digesting and curating mathematical knowledge into a coherent and general form, usable in interactive theorem provers.\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003eTo collectively learn how to make best use of, and improve, interactive theorem provers, such as the Lean language.\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003eTo participate in a community of like-minded researchers on a common project.\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003eTo strengthen trust in the existing mathematical literature, through a combination of audited definitions and theorem statements, and machine verified proofs.\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003eTo build a reusable and open library of formal mathematics, that others can freely build on top of.\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003c/ol\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eTau Ceti is focused primarily on the last point: building an open library, at a quality level sufficient that others can reuse and build on top of it, and at a large enough scale that building on top of it allows downstream projects to reach the research frontier across many areas of mathematics\u003c/strong\u003e (though, as we discuss below, points 4 and 5 matter to us too).\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eTau Ceti is not particularly relevant if you're most interested in 🎉, Bourbaki, and learning how to use and improve Lean itself.\nParticularly on the Bourbaki front, Tau Ceti is not trying to improve the state of the art for curation, clarity, and understanding: those are explicitly human activities, and should happen in libraries like Mathlib and other human-curated downstream libraries. This is what Mathlib excels at, and is rightly proud of its achievements. Similarly, it seems unlikely that we'll learn much about using and improving Lean while working on Tau Ceti besides, hopefully, some scaling problems! Tau Ceti explicitly sets out to follow Mathlib decisions regarding design and use of language features, because these decisions are hard earned through years of expert usage in formalizing mathematics. We think it's essential that as this knowledge evolves in Mathlib and other libraries, Tau Ceti follows and adapts to these lessons.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eWe do hope that Tau Ceti will provide a home for many researchers who want to participate in an active and engaging community. It will be a very different process than contributing to libraries like Mathlib. Primarily, the work is to do high level design: learning to write effective and thorough roadmaps, which efficiently lay out the plans to formalize large areas of mathematics well. We'll need deep mathematical expertise in every subject area, and there's a lot of learning to do about how to write roadmaps that produce the best AI output. We're already getting started on these experiments, and Tau Ceti is an opportunity to build a \u003cem\u003enew community\u003c/em\u003e around a new kind of formalization work.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eSimilarly, we expect that Tau Ceti will help build trust in the existing informal literature. Of course, AI formalized definitions are, a priori, untrustworthy, and a big part of the success criteria for Tau Ceti will be in establishing trust from an initially untrustworthy base. We're working on projects to help rapidly audit deep definition chains. Just as in informal mathematics, trust in results isn't really achieved during initial review. We know that results are true only after they've been integrated into a deeper web of mathematics: we've proved more theorems on top of the definitions, and connected the theory to other parts of mathematics. Our hope is that the scale of Tau Ceti will help us achieve that here, and allow the rapid validation of results \u003cem\u003eby building the theory downstream as well\u003c/em\u003e.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"markdown-heading\" dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003ch2 tabindex=\"-1\" class=\"heading-element\" dir=\"auto\"\u003eCollaboration\u003c/h2\u003e\u003ca id=\"user-content-collaboration\" class=\"anchor\" aria-label=\"Permalink: Collaboration\" href=\"#collaboration\"\u003e\u003csvg data-component=\"Octicon\" class=\"octicon octicon-link\" viewBox=\"0 0 16 16\" version=\"1.1\" width=\"16\" height=\"16\" aria-hidden=\"true\"\u003e\u003cpath d=\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z\"\u003e\u003c/path\u003e\u003c/svg\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eIt's really important we have a good collaboration model with other repositories. We want to make sure that we build rapidly, so that we provide the foundations for people working on frontier research as quickly as possible. But we don't want to get in the way of frontier research, or detract from those efforts.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eThis will be an evolving process, and community input is welcome.\nTo begin with, our plan is to use the \"intentions registration\" mechanism from \u003ca href=\"https://github.com/leanprover-community/intentions\"\u003e\u003ccode\u003eleanprover-community/intentions\u003c/code\u003e\u003c/a\u003e,\nand the shared public registry of intentions at \u003ca href=\"https://github.com/leanprover-community/project-intentions\"\u003e\u003ccode\u003eleanprover-community/project-intentions\u003c/code\u003e\u003c/a\u003e.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eWe already use this mechanism internally so contributors to Tau Ceti can indicate they are actively working on and preparing pull requests for parts of a Tau Ceti roadmap. These intentions are then automatically fed to agents using the \u003ccode\u003e./tauceti\u003c/code\u003e worker exemplar from \u003ca href=\"https://github.com/TauCetiProject/TauCetiWorker\"\u003e\u003ccode\u003eTauCetiProject/TauCetiWorker\u003c/code\u003e\u003c/a\u003e, instructing them to avoid working on roadmap items claimed by others. We hope that contributors implementing their own workers will also use this mechanism.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eWe're working now on extending this mechanism to respect recorded intentions at the public \u003ccode\u003eproject-intentions\u003c/code\u003e registry. Hopefully in future there will also be a federated system of registrations collected from individual downstream projects that Tau Ceti can hook into.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003e(The underlying tooling here was written for earlier projects, but the reusable GitHub Action and the public registry are nice early community benefits from work on Tau Ceti!)\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eWe do want to make some important clarifications about registered intentions, along two axes: frontier vs foundational mathematics, and student projects.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eThere is so much \"frontier mathematics\" (roughly, research papers from the last few years, and those still to come) that Tau Ceti can simply disavow interest here. Tau Ceti wants to build a foundational library, so if someone registers a credible intention to work on a frontier project, Tau Ceti can make sure the relevant results are not included in its roadmaps (or removed if they are already there). This isn't to say that people working on these frontier projects shouldn't simultaneously be contributing to Tau Ceti: we hope they'll direct their AIs to send the foundational material they build to Tau Ceti, and then announce the \"research result\" as an independent downstream project. Indeed, we anticipate that many people working on frontier projects may actually submit Tau Ceti roadmaps for them! On the other hand, for \"foundational mathematics\" (roughly, anything you might expect graduate students at a subject-specific conference to be aware of), we don't intend to wait or remove material from roadmaps. It's important that this gets done, and per the discussion above about what Tau Ceti is and is not, we believe that Tau Ceti \"getting there first\" (reaching the minimal bar of \"correct, formal, and reusable\") does not detract from others curating, digesting, and subsequently incorporating this material into libraries with more ambitious goals.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eThere's a special bar for student projects. It's really important that students are \"left to work in peace\" to the extent possible. We hope that for the most part students are now able to work on frontier projects, and the distinction above does most of the work. But if there are students working on formalizing known mathematics, and it is important to them or their supervisors that no other formalizations exist, we'll try to accommodate. We want to flag for all Tau Ceti contributors that student projects come first: if there's any indication that roadmaps or contributions make use of partially published or announced work by students, or don't respect the registered intentions of student projects, we'll happily just delete material from roadmaps, pull requests, or the main library to make room for students. Common sense will be required here (a student project \"I'm going to do Sobolev spaces\" or \"differential geometry\" obviously isn't going to stop progress at Tau Ceti), and we invite the wider community to participate in getting this right. We recommend using \u003ca href=\"https://leanprover-community/project-intentions\" rel=\"nofollow\"\u003eleanprover-community/project-intentions\u003c/a\u003e to register projects. We would like to encourage everyone to decide that formalizations at the \"reusable only\" level that Tau Ceti aims at are simply not an obstacle to working concurrently on a higher level formalization. But if there are projects where a student would prefer that there are no other formalizations of any kind, we ask that they note this in the intentions registration, or contact us in the Zulip channel for Tau Ceti, and we'll take appropriate action.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"markdown-heading\" dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003ch2 tabindex=\"-1\" class=\"heading-element\" dir=\"auto\"\u003eFinancial model\u003c/h2\u003e\u003ca id=\"user-content-financial-model\" class=\"anchor\" aria-label=\"Permalink: Financial model\" href=\"#financial-model\"\u003e\u003csvg data-component=\"Octicon\" class=\"octicon octicon-link\" viewBox=\"0 0 16 16\" version=\"1.1\" width=\"16\" height=\"16\" aria-hidden=\"true\"\u003e\u003cpath d=\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z\"\u003e\u003c/path\u003e\u003c/svg\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eWe're aware that training and running powerful AIs come at a significant financial and environmental cost. We're also aware that at present the most capable agents are commercial offerings, and consider the question of bridging industrial and academic practices very seriously.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eFor the time being, we will run initial CI and reviews for individual contributors and experiments (we don't really have a budget even for this, but will scrape something together), and expect that contributors will cover the costs of generating the code included in their PRs.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eIt is essential that Tau Ceti remains an open source project, and however inference for generation or review is paid for, the outputs will always be free (as in both speech and beer), protected by the Apache licence.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eWe intend to move to a system where review agents' inference costs are covered by the large scale contributors to the library. This may be in the form of donations (in money or tokens) to the umbrella organizations, or by in-kind inference using sufficiently capable in-house models. We anticipate that individual contributions can be reviewed \"for free\" out of this pool.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eFinally, we understand that participating in AI-assisted mathematics research requires the ability to pay for inference costs, potentially adding a further barrier to entry on top of the existing societal/financial privilege implicit in holding a research position. We're not sure how to respond to this. Possibilities include advocacy for public and private funding, advocacy for capability limitations, and technical capability work on open weight models and cheaper models. Each of these are difficult, have potential adverse effects, and unknown consequences. We hope that everyone involved in Tau Ceti will think hard about these questions, and contribute to meaningful and beneficial solutions.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"markdown-heading\" dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003ch2 tabindex=\"-1\" class=\"heading-element\" dir=\"auto\"\u003eDocumentation\u003c/h2\u003e\u003ca id=\"user-content-documentation\" class=\"anchor\" aria-label=\"Permalink: Documentation\" href=\"#documentation\"\u003e\u003csvg data-component=\"Octicon\" class=\"octicon octicon-link\" viewBox=\"0 0 16 16\" version=\"1.1\" width=\"16\" height=\"16\" aria-hidden=\"true\"\u003e\u003cpath d=\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z\"\u003e\u003c/path\u003e\u003c/svg\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eGenerated API documentation for every declaration in Tau Ceti, hyperlinked into its Mathlib\ndependencies, is published at\n\u003ca href=\"https://taucetiproject.org/docs/\" rel=\"nofollow\"\u003etaucetiproject.org/docs\u003c/a\u003e. It is\nscheduled for regeneration every three hours from \u003ccode\u003emain\u003c/code\u003e with\n\u003ca href=\"https://github.com/leanprover/doc-gen4\"\u003e\u003ccode\u003edoc-gen4\u003c/code\u003e\u003c/a\u003e, alongside the\n\u003ca href=\"https://taucetiproject.org/\" rel=\"nofollow\"\u003eproject website\u003c/a\u003e.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eThe moving \u003ccode\u003edocgen\u003c/code\u003e branch points at the mainline commit whose generated API documentation is\ncurrently published — not necessarily the newest, since a deployment that serves older\ndocumentation moves the branch back to match it. Every successful deployment from \u003ccode\u003emain\u003c/code\u003e attempts\nthat correction, in either direction, so the branch heals rather than drifting indefinitely.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eIt is best-effort rather than exact. Publishing to Pages and updating a git ref cannot be done\natomically, so between the two the branch names documentation that has already been replaced; and\nif the update fails, or the deployment came from a manually dispatched run on another branch, the\nbranch stays wrong — in either direction — until the next deployment from \u003ccode\u003emain\u003c/code\u003e. Anything that\nneeds certainty should instead read\n\u003ca href=\"https://taucetiproject.org/docs/SOURCE_SHA\" rel=\"nofollow\"\u003e\u003ccode\u003e/docs/SOURCE_SHA\u003c/code\u003e\u003c/a\u003e, which is published\ninside the documentation itself and therefore states, at the moment it is read, exactly which commit\nthe live documentation describes.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"markdown-heading\" dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003ch2 tabindex=\"-1\" class=\"heading-element\" dir=\"auto\"\u003eBuilding\u003c/h2\u003e\u003ca id=\"user-content-building\" class=\"anchor\" aria-label=\"Permalink: Building\" href=\"#building\"\u003e\u003csvg data-component=\"Octicon\" class=\"octicon octicon-link\" viewBox=\"0 0 16 16\" version=\"1.1\" width=\"16\" height=\"16\" aria-hidden=\"true\"\u003e\u003cpath d=\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z\"\u003e\u003c/path\u003e\u003c/svg\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"highlight highlight-source-shell notranslate position-relative overflow-auto\" dir=\"auto\" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content=\"lake exe cache get                 # Mathlib's oleans\nbash scripts/lake-cache-get.sh .   # Tau Ceti's own oleans\nlake build\"\u003e\u003cpre\u003elake exe cache get                 \u003cspan class=\"pl-c\"\u003e\u003cspan class=\"pl-c\"\u003e#\u003c/span\u003e Mathlib's oleans\u003c/span\u003e\nbash scripts/lake-cache-get.sh \u003cspan class=\"pl-c1\"\u003e.\u003c/span\u003e   \u003cspan class=\"pl-c\"\u003e\u003cspan class=\"pl-c\"\u003e#\u003c/span\u003e Tau Ceti's own oleans\u003c/span\u003e\nlake build\u003c/pre\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eBoth fetches matter, and they come from different places. \u003ccode\u003elake exe cache get\u003c/code\u003e is Mathlib's own\ncache and covers only Mathlib. Tau Ceti's oleans live in a separate public cache that\n\u003ccode\u003escripts/lake-cache-get.sh\u003c/code\u003e reads, anonymously and with no setup; without that second line\n\u003ccode\u003elake build\u003c/code\u003e compiles the whole library from source, which takes hours. A miss is never fatal: the\nscript discards a partial fetch and leaves you exactly where a from-scratch build would.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eThe cache stores each revision's outputs under the toolchain that built them, and the script looks\nback through ancestors of your checkout to find the nearest published one. So expect a full rebuild\nright after a \u003ccode\u003elean-toolchain\u003c/code\u003e bump, when no ancestor has been published under the new toolchain\nyet, and expect a hit at other times.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"markdown-heading\" dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003ch2 tabindex=\"-1\" class=\"heading-element\" dir=\"auto\"\u003eRoadmaps\u003c/h2\u003e\u003ca id=\"user-content-roadmaps\" class=\"anchor\" aria-label=\"Permalink: Roadmaps\" href=\"#roadmaps\"\u003e\u003csvg data-component=\"Octicon\" class=\"octicon octicon-link\" viewBox=\"0 0 16 16\" version=\"1.1\" width=\"16\" height=\"16\" aria-hidden=\"true\"\u003e\u003cpath d=\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z\"\u003e\u003c/path\u003e\u003c/svg\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eThe roadmaps live in the \u003ca href=\"https://github.com/TauCetiProject/TauCetiRoadmap\"\u003eTauCetiRoadmap\u003c/a\u003e\nrepo: universal covers, the Jacobian challenge, reductive algebraic groups, partial\ndifferential equations, Heegaard Floer and knot Floer homology, and multiquadratic fields and\ngenus theory. When asked to work here, read the roadmap first (see \u003ccode\u003eAGENTS.md\u003c/code\u003e).\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eBefore starting a substantial piece of roadmap work, register and claim your intention so you\ndon't collide with others; see \u003ca href=\"https://github.com/TauCetiProject/TauCetiRoadmap#coordinating-work-intentions-and-claims\"\u003eCoordinating work: intentions and claims\u003c/a\u003e.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"markdown-heading\" dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003ch2 tabindex=\"-1\" class=\"heading-element\" dir=\"auto\"\u003eContributing with the worker CLI\u003c/h2\u003e\u003ca id=\"user-content-contributing-with-the-worker-cli\" class=\"anchor\" aria-label=\"Permalink: Contributing with the worker CLI\" href=\"#contributing-with-the-worker-cli\"\u003e\u003csvg data-component=\"Octicon\" class=\"octicon octicon-link\" viewBox=\"0 0 16 16\" version=\"1.1\" width=\"16\" height=\"16\" aria-hidden=\"true\"\u003e\u003cpath d=\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z\"\u003e\u003c/path\u003e\u003c/svg\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eThe reviews above can be run one PR at a time, but most contribution here happens through a\n\u003cem\u003eworker\u003c/em\u003e. A single round picks one piece of work, does it, and stops; \u003ccode\u003e--loop\u003c/code\u003e runs rounds\nrepeatedly until you interrupt it. The exemplar is\n\u003ca href=\"https://github.com/TauCetiProject/TauCetiWorker\"\u003e\u003ccode\u003eTauCetiProject/TauCetiWorker\u003c/code\u003e\u003c/a\u003e. With\n\u003ca href=\"https://docs.astral.sh/uv/\" rel=\"nofollow\"\u003euv\u003c/a\u003e:\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"highlight highlight-source-shell notranslate position-relative overflow-auto\" dir=\"auto\" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content=\"uv tool install git+https://github.com/TauCetiProject/TauCetiWorker\ngh auth login     # the worker acts as this account, and tends its PRs\ntauceti doctor    # checklist of everything it needs\"\u003e\u003cpre\u003euv tool install git+https://github.com/TauCetiProject/TauCetiWorker\ngh auth login     \u003cspan class=\"pl-c\"\u003e\u003cspan class=\"pl-c\"\u003e#\u003c/span\u003e the worker acts as this account, and tends its PRs\u003c/span\u003e\ntauceti doctor    \u003cspan class=\"pl-c\"\u003e\u003cspan class=\"pl-c\"\u003e#\u003c/span\u003e checklist of everything it needs\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/pre\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003ccode\u003etauceti doctor\u003c/code\u003e is the place to start: it prints a row per prerequisite and tells you what is\nmissing. You need \u003ccode\u003egh\u003c/code\u003e, \u003ccode\u003egit\u003c/code\u003e, \u003ccode\u003euv/uvx\u003c/code\u003e, \u003ccode\u003ejq\u003c/code\u003e, an authenticated \u003ccode\u003egh\u003c/code\u003e, and \u003ccode\u003elake\u003c/code\u003e, plus\ncredentials for whichever agent you run (Codex or Claude). The \u003ccode\u003ebubble\u003c/code\u003e, \u003ccode\u003eincus\u003c/code\u003e, \u003ccode\u003epi\u003c/code\u003e and\n\u003ccode\u003ekiro\u003c/code\u003e rows can stay missing unless you want the sandbox or an alternative agent.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eBy default, agents run with unrestricted host access; see\n\u003ca href=\"https://github.com/TauCetiProject/TauCetiWorker/blob/main/docs/sandbox.md\"\u003esandboxing with \u003ccode\u003e--bubble\u003c/code\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\nfor isolation.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eThen survey before you act:\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"highlight highlight-source-shell notranslate position-relative overflow-auto\" dir=\"auto\" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content=\"tauceti status    # read-only: what work is available, and your quota\ntauceti work      # ONE unit of work, then exit\ntauceti work --loop\"\u003e\u003cpre\u003etauceti status    \u003cspan class=\"pl-c\"\u003e\u003cspan class=\"pl-c\"\u003e#\u003c/span\u003e read-only: what work is available, and your quota\u003c/span\u003e\ntauceti work      \u003cspan class=\"pl-c\"\u003e\u003cspan class=\"pl-c\"\u003e#\u003c/span\u003e ONE unit of work, then exit\u003c/span\u003e\ntauceti work --loop\u003c/pre\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eRun a bare \u003ccode\u003etauceti work\u003c/code\u003e before ever using \u003ccode\u003e--loop\u003c/code\u003e, so you see one complete round end to end.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eEach round prioritizes maintenance and review before new formalization work; see\n\u003ca href=\"https://github.com/TauCetiProject/TauCetiWorker#what-a-round-does\"\u003ethe cascade\u003c/a\u003e.\n\u003ccode\u003etauceti work --dry-run\u003c/code\u003e shows what a round would pick without acting.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eSubscription pacing can be controlled via\n\u003ca href=\"https://github.com/TauCetiProject/TauCetiWorker#pacing-against-quota\"\u003e\u003ccode\u003e--pace\u003c/code\u003e\u003c/a\u003e.\nFor running several workers, see\n\u003ca href=\"https://github.com/TauCetiProject/TauCetiWorker#persistent-workers\"\u003ethe worker documentation\u003c/a\u003e.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"markdown-heading\" dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003ch3 tabindex=\"-1\" class=\"heading-element\" dir=\"auto\"\u003eOnly review\u003c/h3\u003e\u003ca id=\"user-content-only-review\" class=\"anchor\" aria-label=\"Permalink: Only review\" href=\"#only-review\"\u003e\u003csvg data-component=\"Octicon\" class=\"octicon octicon-link\" viewBox=\"0 0 16 16\" version=\"1.1\" width=\"16\" height=\"16\" aria-hidden=\"true\"\u003e\u003cpath d=\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 2.83Z\"\u003e\u003c/path\u003e\u003c/svg\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eIf you would rather review than author:\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"highlight highlight-source-shell notranslate position-relative overflow-auto\" dir=\"auto\" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content=\"tauceti work --loop --only review\"\u003e\u003cpre\u003etauceti work --loop --only review\u003c/pre\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003ccode\u003e--skip roadmap\u003c/code\u003e is \"everything except opening new formalization PRs\" — a good setting if you\nwant to help existing work land.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"markdown-heading\" dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003ch3 tabindex=\"-1\" class=\"heading-element\" dir=\"auto\"\u003eOnly one roadmap 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z\"\u003e\u003c/path\u003e\u003c/svg\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eRoadmap rounds pick a random area each time unless you say otherwise. To steer to one:\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"highlight highlight-source-shell notranslate position-relative overflow-auto\" dir=\"auto\" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content=\"tauceti work --roadmap-only ReductiveGroups\"\u003e\u003cpre\u003etauceti work --roadmap-only ReductiveGroups\u003c/pre\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eThis keeps maintenance and review enabled. We discourage \u003ccode\u003e--only roadmap\u003c/code\u003e: it skips both,\nleaving existing PRs untended while opening new ones.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eThe area is a subdirectory of the \u003ca href=\"https://github.com/TauCetiProject/TauCetiRoadmap\"\u003eTauCetiRoadmap\u003c/a\u003e\nrepo. Conversely \u003ccode\u003e--roadmap-skip AREA[,AREA...]\u003c/code\u003e excludes areas, which is how concurrent workers\ndivide the roadmap between them. Before starting substantial roadmap work, register your\nintention so you do not collide with others — the worker reads the intentions board and avoids\nclaimed targets by default.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eFinally: merging, abandoning and de-duplicating PRs is the repo's CI, not the worker.
